  - The health of the British people was better at the end of the Second World War than at any time before or since.

### 254. [long](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/long_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/lɒŋ/` · **US:** `/lɔːŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lon/long_/long__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lon/long_/long__us_8.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She had long dark hair.
  - He walked down the long corridor.
  - It was the world's longest bridge.
  - a long journey/walk/drive/flight

### 255. [provide](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/provide) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/prəˈvaɪd/` · **US:** `/prəˈvaɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/provi/provide__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/provi/provide__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - provide something Please provide the following information.
  - The exhibition provides an opportunity for local artists to show their work.
  - She did not provide any evidence to substantiate the claims.
  - The hospital has a commitment to provide the best possible medical care.

### 256. [service](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/service_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɜːvɪs/` · **US:** `/ˈsɜːrvɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/ser/servi/service__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/ser/servi/service__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the ambulance/bus/postal service
  - The government aims to improve public services, especially education.
  - Essential services (= the supply of water, gas, electricity) will be maintained.
  - Councillors have called for better provision of services for young people.

### 257. [around](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/around_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈraʊnd/` · **US:** `/əˈraʊnd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/aro/aroun/around__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/aro/aroun/around__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He arrived around five o'clock.
  - The cost would be somewhere around £1 500.
  - There were only around 125 people present.
  - The correct figure is actually around double that amount.

### 258. [friend](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/friend_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/frend/` · **US:** `/frend/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fri/frien/friend__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fri/frien/friend__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - This is my friend Tom.
  - Is he a friend of yours?
  - Jane's a friend of the family.
  - She's an old friend (= I have known her a long time).

### 259. [important](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/important) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪmˈpɔːtnt/` · **US:** `/ɪmˈpɔːrtnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/imp/impor/important__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/imp/impor/important__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an important issue/question/point/factor
  - We have an important decision to make.
  - Money played an important role in his life.
  - Listening is an important part of the job.

### 260. [father](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/father_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɑːðə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈfɑːðər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fat/fathe/father__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fat/fathe/father__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - My father died in 2017.
  - Ben's a wonderful father.
  - Our new boss is a father of three (= he has three children).
  - father to somebody You've been like a father to me.

### 261. [sit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sit) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/sɪt/` · **US:** `/sɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sit/sit__/sit__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sit/sit__/sit__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She sat and stared at the letter in front of her.
  - + adv./prep. May I sit here?
  - Don't just sit there—do something!
  - Just sit still!

### 262. [away](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/away_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈweɪ/` · **US:** `/əˈweɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/awa/away_/away__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/awa/away_/away__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The beach is a mile away.
  - Christmas is still months away.
  - away from somebody/something The president was kept well away from the demonstrators.
  - The station is a few minutes' walk away from here.

### 263. [until](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/until) *(conjunction)*
- **UK:** `/ənˈtɪl/` · **US:** `/ənˈtɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/unt/until/until__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/unt/until/until__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Let's wait until the rain stops.
  - Until she spoke I hadn't realized she wasn't English.
  - You're not going out until you've finished this.
  - Until now I have always lived alone.

### 264. [power](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/power_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpaʊə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈpaʊər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pow/power/power__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pow/power/power__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - power to do something He has the power to make things very unpleasant for us.
  - Religion is losing its power to shape our behaviour.
  - power over somebody/something The aim is to give people more power over their own lives.
  - The government wields enormous power over the economy.

### 265. [hour](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hour) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈaʊə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈaʊər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hou/hour_/hour__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hou/hour_/hour__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I spent an hour on the phone.
  - It will take about an hour to get there.
  - It usually takes me two hours to walk there.
  - The interview lasted half an hour.

### 266. [game](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/game_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɡeɪm/` · **US:** `/ɡeɪm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gam/game_/game__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gam/game_/game__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The kids were playing a game with their balloons.
  - The children invented a new game.
  - a video/computer game
  - I play online games with my friends.

### 267. [often](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/often) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒftən/` · **US:** `/ˈɔːftən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/oft/often/often__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/oft/often/often__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We often go there.
  - I've often wondered what happened to him.
  - How often do you go to the theatre?
  - I see her quite often.

### 268. [yet](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/yet_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/jet/` · **US:** `/jet/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/y/yet/yet__/yet__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/y/yet/yet__/yet__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - (British English) I haven't received a letter from him yet.
  - (North American English) I didn't receive a letter from him yet.
  - not yet ‘Are you ready?’ ‘No, not yet.’
  - We don't yet know what really happened.

### 269. [line](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/line_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/laɪn/` · **US:** `/laɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lin/line_/line__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lin/line_/line__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a straight/solid/dotted/dashed line
  - a wavy/diagonal line
  - a vertical/horizontal line
  - parallel lines

### 270. [political](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/political) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/pəˈlɪtɪkl/` · **US:** `/pəˈlɪtɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pol/polit/political__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pol/polit/political__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a monarch without political power
  - He was a political prisoner (= one who was put in prison for holding opinions that the government thought dangerous).
  - a period of political stability
  - a political situation/crisis

### 271. [end](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/end_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/end/` · **US:** `/end/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/end/end__/end__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/end/end__/end__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's the end of an era.
  - the end of the book
  - at the end of something at the end of the week
  - at the end There’ll be a chance to ask questions at the end.

### 272. [among](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/among) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/əˈmʌŋ/` · **US:** `/əˈmʌŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/amo/among/among__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/amo/among/among__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a house among the trees
  - They strolled among the crowds.
  - I found the letter amongst his papers.
  - It's OK, you're among friends now.

### 273. [ever](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ever) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈevə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈevər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eve/ever_/ever__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eve/ever_/ever__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Nothing ever happens here.
  - Don't you ever get tired?
  - If you're ever in Miami, come and see us.
  - ‘Have you ever been to Rome?’ ‘Yes, I have, actually. Not long ago.’

### 274. [stand](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/stand_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/stænd/` · **US:** `/stænd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sta/stand/stand__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sta/stand/stand__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He has avoided taking a firm stand.
  - stand on something The judge takes a tough stand on corruption.
  - stand against something I admire their principled stand against the war.

### 275. [bad](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bad_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/bæd/` · **US:** `/bæd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bad/bad__/bad__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bad/bad__/bad__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I have some bad news for you, I'm afraid.
  - bad weather/dreams/habits
  - I'm having a really bad day.
  - It was the worst experience of her life.

### 276. [lose](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lose) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/luːz/` · **US:** `/luːz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/los/lose_/lose__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/los/lose_/lose__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I've lost my keys.
  - The tickets seem to have got lost.
  - She lost her husband in the crowd.

### 277. [however](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/however) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/haʊˈevə(r)/` · **US:** `/haʊˈevər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/how/howev/however__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/how/howev/however__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He was feeling bad. He went to work, however, and tried to concentrate.
  - We thought the figures were correct. However, we have now discovered some errors.

### 278. [member](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/member) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmembə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈmembər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mem/membe/member__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mem/membe/member__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - member of something a member of the family/community
  - He is an important member of our team.
  - a member of the human race
  - Members of the public are invited to come and view the work

### 279. [pay](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pay_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/peɪ/` · **US:** `/peɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pay/pay__/pay__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pay/pay__/pay__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Her job is hard work, but the pay is good.
  - workers on low pay
  - overtime/holiday pay
  - Her monthly take-home pay after taxes is $2 600.

### 280. [law](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/law) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/lɔː/` · **US:** `/lɔː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/law/law__/law__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/law/law__/law__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to pass a law (= officially make it part of the system of laws)
  - law against (doing) something Police don't have the resources to enforce the law against dumping waste.
  - (informal) There ought to be a law against it!
  - law on something The company's actions violated the law on equal employment.

### 281. [meet](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/meet_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/miːt/` · **US:** `/miːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mee/meet_/meet__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mee/meet_/meet__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Maybe we'll meet again some time.
  - meet somebody Did you meet anyone in town?
  - I've never met anyone like her.
  - A year or so later I happened to meet him again.

### 282. [car](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/car) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kɑː(r)/` · **US:** `/kɑːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/car/car__/car__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/car/car__/car__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Paula got into the car and drove off.
  - by car How did you come?’ ‘By car.’
  - in a/the car Are you going in the car?
  - Her husband was driving the car at the time of the accident.

### 283. [city](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/city) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɪti/` · **US:** `/ˈsɪti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cit/city_/city__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cit/city_/city__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Parking is difficult in the city centre.
  - It is one of the world's most beautiful cities.
  - Riots occurred in all the major cities.
  - the country’s capital city

### 284. [almost](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/almost) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɔːlməʊst/` · **US:** `/ˈɔːlməʊst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/alm/almos/almost__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/alm/almos/almost__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I like almost all of them.
  - They'll eat almost anything.
  - Almost no one (= hardly anyone) believed him.
  - The castle is almost entirely surrounded by water.

### 285. [include](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/include) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈkluːd/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈkluːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inc/inclu/include__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inc/inclu/include__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - include something The tour included a visit to the Science Museum.
  - Does the price include tax?
  - The report includes a number of recommendations.
  - include doing something Your duties will include greeting visitors and directing them to the appropriate department.

### 286. [continue](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/continue) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈtɪnjuː/` · **US:** `/kənˈtɪnjuː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontinue__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontinue__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - If the current trend continues, that number will increase 165 per cent by 2050.
  - Police have not yet made any arrests, but investigations continue.
  - + adv./prep. The exhibition continues until 25 July.
  - Fighting continued for four years.

### 287. [set](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/set_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/set/` · **US:** `/set/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/set/set__/set__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/set/set__/set__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a set of six chairs
  - a complete set of her novels
  - a set of false teeth
  - These companies operate under a strict set of rules.

### 288. [later](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/later_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈleɪtə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈleɪtər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lat/later/later__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lat/later/later__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Rembrandt's later works
  - This is discussed in more detail in a later chapter.
  - The judge will give her ruling at a later date.
  - The match has been postponed to a later date.

### 289. [community](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/community) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈmjuːnəti/` · **US:** `/kəˈmjuːnəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/commu/community__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/commu/community__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The local community supported us from the start.
  - the international community (= the countries of the world as a group)
  - It is hoped that the campaign will encourage members of the community to get active.
  - the problems faced by rural communities

### 290. [much](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/much_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/mʌtʃ/` · **US:** `/mʌtʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/muc/much_/much__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/muc/much_/much__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Thank you very much for the flowers.
  - I would very much like to see you again.
  - He isn't in the office much (= often).
  - You worry too much.

### 291. [name](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/name_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/neɪm/` · **US:** `/neɪm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nam/name_/name__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nam/name_/name__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What's your name?
  - What is/was the name, please? (= a polite way of asking somebody’s name)
  - Please write your full name and address below.
  - Leave your name and number (= phone number) and we'll call you later.

### 292. [five](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/five) *(number)*
- **UK:** `/faɪv/` · **US:** `/faɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fiv/five_/five__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fiv/five_/five__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There are only five cookies left.
  - five of Sweden’s top financial experts

  - Do you have change for five dollars?

### 293. [once](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/once_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/wʌns/` · **US:** `/wʌns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/onc/once_/once__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/onc/once_/once__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I've only been there once.
  - He cleans his car once a week.
  - She only sees her parents once every six months.

### 294. [white](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/white_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/waɪt/` · **US:** `/waɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/whi/white/white_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/whi/white/white__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a crisp white shirt
  - a white dress
  - a plant with beautiful white flowers

### 295. [least](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/least_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/liːst/` · **US:** `/liːst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lea/least/least__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lea/least/least__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He always turns up just when you least expect him.
  - She chose the least expensive of the hotels.
  - I never hid the truth, least of all from you.
  - We had to settle for the least worst option (= the best of several options that were all bad).

### 296. [president](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/president) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprezɪdənt/` · **US:** `/ˈprezɪdənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/presi/president__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/presi/president__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - the President of the United States

  - Do you have any comment, Mr President?

### 297. [learn](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/learn) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/lɜːn/` · **US:** `/lɜːrn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lea/learn/learn__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lea/learn/learn__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - learn something to learn a language/skill/trade
  - He had the opportunity to learn English in Australia.
  - learn (something) from somebody/something I learned a lot from my father.
  - Everyone in the class had the opportunity to learn from each other.

### 298. [real](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/real_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/rɪəl/` · **US:** `/ˈriːəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rea/real_/real__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rea/real_/real__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a real danger/risk/threat/concern

  - It wasn't a ghost; it was a real person.
  - pictures of animals, both real and mythological

### 299. [change](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/change_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tʃeɪndʒ/` · **US:** `/tʃeɪndʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cha/chang/change__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cha/chang/change__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - change in something We're hoping for a change in the weather.
  - There was no change in the patient's condition overnight.
  - I need to make some major changes in my life.
  - a change in policy/law

### 300. [team](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/team_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tiːm/` · **US:** `/tiːm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tea/team_/team__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tea/team_/team__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a football/basketball/soccer team

### 305. [kid](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/kid_1) *(noun)*

### 307. [information](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/information) *(noun)*

- **Examples:**

- **Examples:**

  - I'll see whether she's at home (= or not at home).

  - a back room (= one at the back of a building)
  - My phone's in my back pocket.

### 316. [watch](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/watch_2) *(noun)*

- **Examples:**
  - She kept looking anxiously at her watch.

### 317. [together](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/together_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/təˈɡeðə(r)/` · **US:** `/təˈɡeðər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tog/toget/together__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tog/toget/together__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We grew up together.
  - Get all the ingredients together before you start cooking.
  - Stay close together—I don't want anyone to get lost.
  - Do you want to get together (= meet) again next week?

### 318. [follow](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/follow) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɒləʊ/` · **US:** `/ˈfɑːləʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fol/follo/follow__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fol/follo/follow__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - follow somebody/something Follow me please.

  - Wherever she led, they followed.

### 319. [around](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/around_2) *(adverb)*

### 324. [anything](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/anything) *(pronoun)*

- **Examples:**

  - If you remember anything at all, please let us know.
  - We hope to prevent anything unpleasant from happening.

### 325. [create](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/create) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kriˈeɪt/` · **US:** `/kriˈeɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cre/creat/create__gb_5.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cre/creat/create__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Scientists disagree about how the universe was created.
  - The main purpose of industry is to create wealth.
  - The government plans to create more jobs for young people.
  - Create a new directory and put all your files into it.

### 326. [public](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/public_1) *(adjective)*

- **Examples:**
  - The campaign is designed to increase public awareness of the issues.
  - Levels of waste from the factory may be a danger to public health.
  - Public safety must come first.
  - Why would the closure of hospitals be in the public interest (= useful to ordinary people)?

### 327. [already](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/already) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ɔːlˈredi/` · **US:** `/ɔːlˈredi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/alr/alrea/already__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/alr/alrea/already__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ‘Lunch?’ ‘No thanks, I've already eaten.’
  - We got there early but Mike had already left.
  - By 2015, filming of the sequel was already under way.
  - Much of what he said I knew already.

### 328. [speak](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/speak_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/spiːk/` · **US:** `/spiːk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/spe/speak/speak__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/spe/speak/speak__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - speak to somebody The President refused to speak to reporters.
  - speak about something We never spoke about her illness.
  - The confidential setting allowed us to speak freely about our concerns.
  - speak to somebody about something/somebody I've spoken to the manager about it.

- [Oxford Learner's Dictionary Search](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/others)

### 330. [read](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/read_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/riːd/` · **US:** `/riːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rea/read_/read__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rea/read_/read__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She's still learning to read.
  - Some children can read and write before they go to school.
  - read something I can't read your writing.
  - Can you read music?

### 331. [level](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/level_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlevl/` · **US:** `/ˈlevl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lev/level/level__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lev/level/level__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Pitch the tent on level ground.
  - Add a level tablespoon of flour (= enough to fill the spoon but not so much that it goes above the level of the edge of the spoon).

### 332. [allow](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/allow) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈlaʊ/` · **US:** `/əˈlaʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/all/allow/allow__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/all/allow/allow__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - allow somebody/something to do something His parents won't allow him to stay out late.
  - He allowed his mind to wander.
  - The law allowed companies to dismiss workers without any reason.
  - be allowed to do something He is not allowed to stay out late.

### 333. [add](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/add_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/æd/` · **US:** `/æd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/add/add__/add__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/add/add__/add__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - add something Next add the flour.
  - The juice contains no added sugar.
  - As an added bonus, the book includes many black-and-white photographs.
  - Melt the butter, then add the onion.

### 334. [office](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/office) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒfɪs/` · **US:** `/ˈɑːfɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/off/offic/office__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/off/offic/office__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The company is moving to new offices on the other side of town.
  - Are you going to the office today?
  - in the office I'm sorry, Mr Anders is not in the office today.
  - at the office I've had a tough day at the office.

### 335. [spend](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/spend_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/spend/` · **US:** `/spend/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/spe/spend/spend__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/spe/spend/spend__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - spend something I've spent all my money already.
  - spend something on something She spent £100 on a new dress.
  - spend something on doing something That money would be better spent on educating children.
  - spend something doing something The company has spent thousands of pounds updating their computer systems.

### 336. [door](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/door) *(noun)*

- **Examples:**
  - What sort of person would do a thing like that?
  - He's a fascinating person.

  - He's just the person we need for the job.

### 339. [art](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/art_1) *(noun)*

### 340. [sure](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sure_1) *(adjective)*

- **Examples:**
  - ‘Is that John over there?’ ‘I'm not sure.’
  - You don't sound very sure.
  - sure (that)… I'm pretty sure (that) he'll agree.
  - Are you sure you don't mind?

### 341. [such](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/such) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/sʌtʃ/` · **US:** `/sʌtʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/suc/such_/such__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/suc/such_/such__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - This issue was of such importance that we could not afford to ignore it.
  - such a/an… Why are you in such a hurry?
  - It's such a beautiful day!
  - such is something that… (formal) Such is the elegance of this typeface that it is still a favourite of designers.

### 342. [war](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/war) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/wɔː(r)/` · **US:** `/wɔːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/war/war__/war__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/war/war__/war__us_1.mp3)

### 344. [party](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/party_1) *(noun)*

### 347. [result](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/result_1) *(noun)*

- **Examples:**

  - She's old enough to decide for herself.
  - We didn't leave early enough.

### 368. [both](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/both) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/bəʊθ/` · **US:** `/bəʊθ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bot/both_/both__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bot/both_/both__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Both women were French.
  - Both the women were French.
  - Both of the women were French.
  - I talked to the women. Both of them were French/They were both French.

### 369. [education](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/education) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌedʒuˈkeɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌedʒuˈkeɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/edu/educa/education__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/edu/educa/education__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - primary/elementary education
  - post-secondary education
  - a college/university education
  - She completed her formal education in 2019.

### 370. [across](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/across_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈkrɒs/` · **US:** `/əˈkrɔːs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/acr/acros/across__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/acr/acros/across__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### 371. [although](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/although) *(conjunction)*
- **UK:** `/ɔːlˈðəʊ/` · **US:** `/ɔːlˈðəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/alt/altho/although__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/alt/altho/although__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Although the sun was shining, it wasn't very warm.
  - Although small, the kitchen is well designed.

### 372. [remember](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/remember) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈmembə(r)/` · **US:** `/rɪˈmembər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rem/remem/remember__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rem/remem/remember__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - remember somebody/something This is Carla. Do you remember her?
  - I don't remember my first day at school.
  - I'll always remember this holiday.
  - She doesn't remember a thing about it.

### 373. [foot](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/foot_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/fʊt/` · **US:** `/fʊt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/foo/foot_/foot__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/foo/foot_/foot__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - My feet are aching.
  - She kicked the ball with her right foot.
  - He's broken several bones in his left foot.
  - The whole audience rose to its feet (= stood up) and cheered.

### 374. [second](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/second1_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsekənd/` · **US:** `/ˈsekənd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sec/secon/second__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sec/secon/second__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She came second in the marathon.
  - One of the smaller parties came a close second (= nearly won).
  - The women's team came in fourth and the men finished second.
  - I agreed to speak second.

### 375. [boy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/boy_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/bɔɪ/` · **US:** `/bɔɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/boy/boy__/boy__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/boy/boy__/boy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a little/small/young boy
  - I used to play here as a boy.
  - The older boys at school used to tease him.
  - Now she's a teenager, she's starting to be interested in boys.

### 376. [maybe](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/maybe_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmeɪbi/` · **US:** `/ˈmeɪbi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/may/maybe/maybe__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/may/maybe/maybe__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Maybe he'll come, maybe he won't.
  - ‘Are you going to sell your house?’ ‘Maybe.’
  - Is she right? Maybe, maybe not.
  - Maybe it will do me some good to go for a run.

### 377. [toward](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/toward)
- [Oxford Learner's Dictionary Search](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/toward)

### 378. [able](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/able_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈeɪbl/` · **US:** `/ˈeɪbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/abl/able_/able__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/abl/able_/able__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You must be able to speak French for this job.
  - A viral illness left her barely able to walk.
  - We're still able to get visas to come and go from Thailand.
  - I didn't feel able to disagree with him.

### 379. [age](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/age_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/eɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/eɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/age/age__/age__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/age/age__/age__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You're the same age as my brother.
  - When I was your age I was already married.
  - ways of calculating the age of the earth
  - to reach retirement age

### 380. [off](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/off_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ɒf/` · **US:** `/ɔːf/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/off/off__/off__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/off/off__/off__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He's had his beard shaved off.
  - Take your coat off.
  - The label must have fallen off.
  - Don't leave the toothpaste with the top off.

### 381. [policy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/policy) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpɒləsi/` · **US:** `/ˈpɑːləsi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pol/polic/policy__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pol/polic/policy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - policy on something the present government’s policy on education
  - The company has adopted a firm policy on shoplifting.
  - The new managers are expected to implement new policies.
  - policy of something We have tried to pursue a policy of neutrality.

### 382. [everything](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/everything) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈevriθɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈevriθɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eve/every/everything__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eve/every/everything__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Everything had gone.
  - When we confronted him, he denied everything.
  - Take this bag, and leave everything else to me.
  - She seemed to have everything—looks, money, intelligence.

### 383. [love](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/love_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/lʌv/` · **US:** `/lʌv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lov/love_/love__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lov/love_/love__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She has earned the love and respect of many people.
  - love for somebody/something a mother’s unconditional love for her children
  - love of somebody/something love of your country

### 384. [process](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/process1_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprəʊses/` · **US:** `/ˈprɑːses/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/proce/process__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/proce/process__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the consultation/planning process
  - Each time we have to go through the whole decision-making process again.
  - Repeat this process until the entire area is tiled.
  - I'm afraid getting things changed will be a slow process.

### 385. [music](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/music) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmjuːzɪk/` · **US:** `/ˈmjuːzɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mus/music/music__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mus/music/music__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I like any kind of pop or dance music.
  - He plays traditional Korean music.
  - People often choose to listen to music that suits their moods and activities.
  - She could hear music playing somewhere.

### 386. [including](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/including) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈkluːdɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈkluːdɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inc/inclu/including__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inc/inclu/including__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I've got three days' holiday including New Year's Day.
  - Six people were killed in the riot, including a policeman.
  - It's £7.50, not including tax.

### 387. [consider](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/consider) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈsɪdə(r)/` · **US:** `/kənˈsɪdər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsider__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsider__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I'd like some time to consider.
  - consider something She is considering her options.
  - Let us consider the facts.
  - We are considering various possibilities.

### 388. [appear](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/appear) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈpɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/əˈpɪr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/app/appea/appear__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/app/appea/appear__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Three days later a rash appeared.
  - Symptoms usually start appearing within two to three days.
  - + adv./prep. A cat suddenly appeared out of nowhere.
  - Smoke appeared on the horizon.

### 389. [actually](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/actually) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈæktʃuəli/` · **US:** `/ˈæktʃuəli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/act/actua/actually__gb_5.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/act/actua/actually__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What did she actually say?
  - It's not actually raining now.
  - The book was never actually published.
  - I'm glad we were actually able to get the message across.

### 390. [buy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/buy_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/baɪ/` · **US:** `/baɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/buy/buy__/buy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/buy/buy__/buy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - buy (something) Where did you buy that dress?
  - She had no money to buy a ticket.
  - They can't afford to buy school books.
  - If you're thinking of getting a new car, now is a good time to buy.

### 391. [probably](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/probably) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprɒbəbli/` · **US:** `/ˈprɑːbəbli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/proba/probably__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/proba/probably__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You're probably right.
  - I think that's probably true.
  - It'll probably be OK.
  - It was the best known and probably the most popular of her songs.

### 392. [human](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/human_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhjuːmən/` · **US:** `/ˈhjuːmən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hum/human/human__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hum/human/human__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the human body/brain

### 393. [wait](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wait_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/weɪt/` · **US:** `/weɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wai/wait_/wait__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wai/wait_/wait__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The wait seemed interminable.
  - It took six months for the house to be finished, but it was worth the wait.
  - wait for somebody/something We had a long wait for the bus.
  - He now faces an agonizing two-month wait for the test results.

### 394. [serve](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/serve_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/sɜːv/` · **US:** `/sɜːrv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/ser/serve/serve__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/ser/serve/serve__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - serve (something) Breakfast is served between 7 and 10 a.m.
  - When do you stop serving hot food?
  - Pour the sauce over the pasta and serve immediately.
  - Shall I serve?

- **UK:** `/ˈmɑːkɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈmɑːrkɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mar/marke/market__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mar/marke/market__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a fruit/flower/fish market
  - an antiques market
  - an indoor/a street market
  - market stalls/traders

### 396. [die](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/die_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/daɪ/` · **US:** `/daɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/die/die__/die__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/die/die__/die__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - My father died suddenly at the age of 48.
  - At least six people have died in the accident.
  - That plant's going to die if you don't water it!
  - He later died in hospital.

### 397. [send](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/send) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/send/` · **US:** `/send/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sen/send_/send__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sen/send_/send__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - send something She sent a letter of support.
  - Let me just send this email.
  - (British English) to send something by post
  - (North American English) to send something by mail

### 398. [expect](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/expect) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪkˈspekt/` · **US:** `/ɪkˈspekt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exp/expec/expect__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exp/expec/expect__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - expect something The company is expecting record sales this year.
  - Change often happens when you least expect it.
  - expect something from somebody/something Don't expect sympathy from me!
  - expect something of somebody/something That's not the sort of behaviour I expect of you!

### 399. [home](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/home_3) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/həʊm/` · **US:** `/həʊm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hom/home_/home__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hom/home_/home__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - home life (= with your family)
  - a person’s home address/country
  - We offer customers a free home delivery service.

### 400. [sense](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sense_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/sens/` · **US:** `/sens/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sen/sense/sense__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sen/sense/sense__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - One of the most important things in a partner is a sense of humour (= the ability to find things funny or make people laugh).
  - He has a very good sense of direction (= finds the way to a place easily).
  - (figurative) She has lost all sense of direction in her life (= the idea of what she should do in her life).
  - Always try to keep a sense of proportion (= of the relative importance of different things).

## Words 401 – 500

### 401. [build](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/build_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/bɪld/` · **US:** `/bɪld/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bui/build/build__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bui/build/build__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - build (something) They have permission to build 200 new homes.
  - to build a plant/facility/structure
  - We build computer systems for large companies.
  - Robins build nests almost anywhere.

### 402. [stay](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/stay_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/steɪ/` · **US:** `/steɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sta/stay_/stay__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sta/stay_/stay__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I enjoyed my stay in Prague.
  - an overnight stay
  - In recent years the average hospital stay for elderly patients has decreased.

### 403. [fall](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fall_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/fɔːl/` · **US:** `/fɔːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fal/fall_/fall__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fal/fall_/fall__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I had a bad fall and broke my arm.
  - fall from something She was killed in a fall from a horse.

### 404. [oh](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/oh_1) *(exclamation)*
- **UK:** `/əʊ/` · **US:** `/əʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/owe/owe__/owe__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/oh_/oh__u/oh__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ‘I saw Ben yesterday.’ ‘Oh yes, how is he?’
  - Oh dear! What's happened now?
  - You can’t come tonight? Oh well, see you next week then.
  - ‘Emma has a new job.’ ‘Oh, has she?’

### 405. [nation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈneɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˈneɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nat/natio/nation__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nat/natio/nation__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - European/Arab/Asian nations
  - The West African nation of Togo has presidential elections this week.
  - Developing nations need affordable access to drugs and medication.
  - Fewer and fewer people in industrialized nations actually know where their food comes from.

### 406. [plan](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/plan_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/plæn/` · **US:** `/plæn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pla/plan_/plan__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pla/plan_/plan__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - plan for something Do you have any plans for the summer?
  - plan to do something There are no plans to build new offices.
  - Your best plan (= the best thing to do) would be to go by car.
  - There's been a change of plan.

### 407. [cut](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cut_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kʌt/` · **US:** `/kʌt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cut/cut__/cut__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cut/cut__/cut__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - cuts and bruises on the face
  - Blood poured from the deep cut on his arm.

### 408. [college](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/college) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒlɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːlɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/col/colle/college__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/col/colle/college__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a secretarial college
  - the Royal College of Art
  - a college course/student
  - She wanted to go to college to become a nurse.

### 409. [interest](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/interest_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪntrest/` · **US:** `/ˈɪntrest/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/inter/interest__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/inter/interest__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Her research has generated interest around the world.
  - The case has attracted the interest of the public and media.
  - interest in something Do your parents take an interest in your friends?
  - By that time I had lost (all) interest in the idea.

### 410. [death](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/death) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/deθ/` · **US:** `/deθ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dea/death/death__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dea/death/death__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the anniversary of his wife’s death
  - his sudden/untimely/premature death
  - the tragic death of a child
  - The jury returned a verdict of accidental death.

### 411. [course](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/course_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kɔːs/` · **US:** `/kɔːrs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cou/cours/course__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cou/cours/course__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a French/chemistry course
  - course in something He took a course in Russian language and literature.
  - I want to do a course in art and design.
  - The department offers short courses in drama, fiction, and poetry.

### 412. [someone](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/someone) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsʌmwʌn/` · **US:** `/ˈsʌmwʌn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/som/someo/someone__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/som/someo/someone__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There's someone at the door.
  - Someone's left their bag behind.
  - It's time for someone new (= a new person) to take over.
  - It couldn't have been me—it must have been someone else (= a different person).

### 413. [experience](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/experience_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪkˈspɪəriəns/` · **US:** `/ɪkˈspɪriəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exp/exper/experience__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exp/exper/experience__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - My lack of practical experience was a disadvantage.
  - experience of something Do you have any previous experience of this type of work?
  - This new approach draws on years of experience of teaching children to read.
  - experience in doing something a doctor with experience in dealing with patients suffering from stress

### 414. [behind](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/behind_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/bɪˈhaɪnd/` · **US:** `/bɪˈhaɪnd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/beh/behin/behind__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/beh/behin/behind__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She rode off down the road with the dog running behind.
  - The others are a long way behind.
  - He was shot from behind as he ran away.
  - I had fallen so far behind that it seemed pointless trying to catch up.

### 415. [reach](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/reach_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/riːtʃ/` · **US:** `/riːtʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rea/reach/reach__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rea/reach/reach__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - beyond somebody's reach The shot was well beyond the reach of the goalkeeper.
  - out of somebody's reach Keep all medicines out of reach of children.
  - within somebody's reach He lashed out angrily, hitting anyone within his reach.
  - It's a book I like to keep within arm's reach.

### 416. [local](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/local_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈləʊkl/` · **US:** `/ˈləʊkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/loc/local/local__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/loc/local/local__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - local people/residents/businesses
  - members of the local community
  - the local police
  - local elections

### 417. [kill](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/kill_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kɪl/` · **US:** `/kɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/k/kil/kill_/kill__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/k/kil/kill_/kill__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - kill (somebody/something) Cancer kills thousands of people every year.
  - Tiredness while driving can kill.
  - Dozens of civilians were killed or injured in the attack.
  - She was nearly killed by a car bomb.

### 418. [six](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/six) *(number)*
- **UK:** `/sɪks/` · **US:** `/sɪks/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/six/six__/six__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/six/six__/six__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There are six cookies left.
  - six of Sweden’s top financial experts
  - Ten people were invited but only six turned up.
  - Can you lend me six dollars?

### 419. [remain](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/remain) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈmeɪn/` · **US:** `/rɪˈmeɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rem/remai/remain__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rem/remai/remain__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - remain + adj. to remain silent
  - to remain standing/seated/motionless
  - to remain calm/confident
  - Train fares are likely to remain unchanged.

### 420. [effect](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/effect_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈfekt/` · **US:** `/ɪˈfekt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eff/effec/effect__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eff/effec/effect__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the beneficial effects of exercise
  - What are the long-term effects of this treatment?
  - The results show a statistically significant effect.
  - effect on somebody/something Her tears had no effect on him.

### 421. [use](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/use_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/juːs/` · **US:** `/juːs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/use/use__/use__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/use/use__/use__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - A ban was imposed on the use of chemical weapons.
  - You can't justify the use of military force.
  - I'm not sure that this is the most valuable use of my time.
  - for… use These videos are intended for classroom use.

### 422. [yeah](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/yeah) *(exclamation)*
- **UK:** `/jeə/` · **US:** `/jeə/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/y/yea/yeah_/yeah__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/y/yea/yeah_/yeah__us_1.mp3)

### 423. [suggest](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/suggest) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/səˈdʒest/` · **US:** `/səɡˈdʒest/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sug/sugge/suggest__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sug/sugge/suggest__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - suggest something I'd like to suggest a different explanation for the company's decline.
  - suggest something to somebody The designer is known for suggesting innovative ideas to her clients.
  - suggest itself He thought long and hard, but no answer suggested itself.
  - suggest itself to somebody A solution immediately suggested itself to me (= I immediately thought of a solution).

### 424. [class](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/class_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/klɑːs/` · **US:** `/klæs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cla/class/class__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cla/class/class__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in the/your class We were in the same class at school.
  - She is the youngest in her class.
  - He came top of the class.
  - The whole class was/were told to stay behind after school.

### 425. [control](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/control_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈtrəʊl/` · **US:** `/kənˈtrəʊl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontrol__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontrol__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - control of somebody/something The party expects to gain control of the council in the next election.
  - Militants have taken control of the town.
  - The Democrats have lost control of Congress.
  - A military junta seized control of the country.

### 426. [raise](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/raise_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/reɪz/` · **US:** `/reɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rai/raise/raise__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rai/raise/raise__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She raised the gun and fired.
  - He raised a hand in greeting.
  - She raised her eyes from her work.
  - raise something up He slowly raised his head up.

### 427. [care](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/care_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/keə(r)/` · **US:** `/ker/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/car/care_/care__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/car/care_/care__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Some people were badly injured and needed medical care.
  - We aim to continually improve the quality of patient care.
  - How much do men share housework and the care of the children?
  - care for somebody/something to provide care for the elderly

### 428. [perhaps](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/perhaps) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/præps/` · **US:** `/pərˈhæps/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/per/perha/perhaps__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/per/perha/perhaps__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ‘Are you going to come?’ ‘Perhaps. I'll see how I feel.’
  - Perhaps he's forgotten.

### 429. [little](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/little_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlɪtl/` · **US:** `/ˈlɪtl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lit/littl/little__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lit/littl/little__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a little house
  - a little group of tourists
  - a little old lady
  - We passed through several nice little towns.

### 430. [late](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/late_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/leɪt/` · **US:** `/leɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lat/late_/late__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lat/late_/late__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I'm sorry I'm late.
  - late for something She's late for work every day.
  - My flight was an hour late.
  - We apologize for the late arrival of this train.

### 431. [hard](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hard_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/hɑːd/` · **US:** `/hɑːrd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/har/hard_/hard__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/har/hard_/hard__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a hard choice/decision/question
  - hard to do something It is hard to believe that she's only nine.
  - It's hard to see how they can lose.
  - It's hard to imagine a more cynical political strategy.

### 432. [field](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/field_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/fiːld/` · **US:** `/fiːld/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fie/field/field__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fie/field/field__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We had to walk across a ploughed field.
  - in a field We camped in a field near the village.
  - People were working in the fields.
  - field of something We saw golden fields of wheat.

### 433. [else](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/else) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/els/` · **US:** `/els/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/els/else_/else__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/els/else_/else__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What else did he say?
  - I don't want anything else, thanks.
  - I'm taking a few clothes and some books, not much else.

### 434. [pass](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pass_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pɑːs/` · **US:** `/pæs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pas/pass_/pass__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pas/pass_/pass__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a boarding pass (= for a plane)

  - We had backstage passes to the show.

### 435. [former](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/former_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɔːmə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈfɔːrmər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/for/forme/former__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/for/forme/former__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - her former life as a Russian ballerina

### 436. [sell](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sell_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/sel/` · **US:** `/sel/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sel/sell_/sell__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sel/sell_/sell__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - sell (something) I recently sold my bike.
  - His father went bankrupt and the family had to sell their home.
  - The board agreed to sell the company.
  - The shares were sold in the open market.

### 437. [major](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/major_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmeɪdʒə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈmeɪdʒər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/maj/major/major__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/maj/major/major__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a major road
  - Some major international companies refused to do business with them.
  - He played a major role in setting up the system.
  - major sporting events

### 438. [sometimes](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sometimes) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsʌmtaɪmz/` · **US:** `/ˈsʌmtaɪmz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/som/somet/sometimes__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/som/somet/sometimes__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Sometimes I go by car.
  - He sometimes writes to me.
  - I like to be on my own sometimes.
  - The skiing season runs from October to late April, and sometimes even into May.

### 439. [require](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/require) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈkwaɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/rɪˈkwaɪər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/req/requi/require__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/req/requi/require__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - require something These pets require a lot of care and attention.
  - Deciphering the code requires an expert.
  - This condition requires urgent treatment.
  - Do you require anything else? (= in a shop/store, for example)

### 440. [along](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/along_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈlɒŋ/` · **US:** `/əˈlɔːŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/alo/along/along__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/alo/along/along__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I was just walking along singing to myself.
  - He pointed out various landmarks as we drove along.

### 441. [development](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/development) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈveləpmənt/` · **US:** `/dɪˈveləpmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dev/devel/development__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dev/devel/development__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a baby’s development in the womb

- [Oxford Learner's Dictionary Search](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/themself)

### 443. [report](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/report_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈpɔːt/` · **US:** `/rɪˈpɔːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rep/repor/report__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rep/repor/report__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Are these news reports true?
  - media/press/newspaper reports
  - report on something And now over to Jim Muir, for a report on the South African election.
  - A local news station aired a special report on the controversy.

### 444. [role](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/role) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/rəʊl/` · **US:** `/rəʊl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rol/role_/role__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rol/role_/role__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She has landed the lead role (= the most important one) in a new play.
  - a leading/starring role
  - the role of somebody He played the role of Sonny in ‘The Godfather’.

### 445. [better](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/better_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbetə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈbetər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bet/bette/better__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bet/bette/better__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We're hoping for better weather tomorrow.
  - Her work is getting better and better.
  - He is in a much better mood than usual.
  - The meal couldn't have been better.

### 446. [economic](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/economic) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌekəˈnɒmɪk/` · **US:** `/ˌekəˈnɑːmɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eco/econo/economic__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eco/econo/economic__us_2_rr.mp3)
